CBC_v4_74.pine · v6
// =====================================================================================
// 08/05/2026: v4.00 - Still over by 180 (100436/100256) after v3.99 — very close, small safe trim. Checked f_cbc_state() (called 7 times) as a candidate for the same hoist treatment as f_ltf_htf_mtf_order()/chart_tf_disp() — but each call sits inside a DIFFERENT request.security() context (current chart, HTF, MTF), so they're genuinely different evaluations, not redundant duplicates; left alone rather than risk breaking the HTF/MTF CBC pipeline for no real gain. Instead removed 26 tooltips entirely (not just shortened) where the tooltip purely restated what the label + inline color/size context already made obvious — mostly single-purpose color pickers (VWAP/EMA200/YDH/YDL/PMH/PML/session H-L lines, LTF/HTF/MTF pivot H/L colors) and a few font-size/anchor settings. Saved 931 characters. Verified no dangling commas or broken call syntax at any of the 26 edit sites.
// 08/05/2026: v4.01 - IMPORTANT FINDING: v4.00's 931-char tooltip removal produced the EXACT SAME token count (100436, not one token different) — clean confirmation that tooltip STRING CONTENT doesn't count toward CE10117 at all, the same way comments were confirmed not to back at v3.72. This means every tooltip-shortening pass done from v3.75 onward accomplished nothing for this specific error (still worth doing for the settings-panel UI itself, just not for this budget). Tried hoisting f_market_session_et() (called 4x, same category of fix as the last 3 successful hoists) to a single mkt_now variable — but this one actually made things WORSE (100436 -> 100450, up by 14), unlike the other 3 which all tested lower. Reverted it completely back to 4 independent calls (confirmed: byte-for-byte the same as before this attempt, right down to the "string" type prefixes) rather than defend a change with unclear-at-best results. Lesson: not every "N redundant-looking calls" pattern is actually free to consolidate — this one likely interacts with Pine's function-inlining or scope-capture in a way that costs more than it saves when reaching into a function-local scope (mkt_tbl, inside f_render_status_table()) from a top-level variable, unlike the previous 3 hoists which stayed within scopes that didn't have that same cross-boundary read.
// 08/05/2026: v4.02 - Switched from consolidation to dead-code removal after v4.01's hoist backfired. Systematically scanned for variables assigned once and never read anywhere else again. Removed 3 genuinely dead pieces: mtf_live_t_close (an entire unused request.security() call — the LTF/HTF equivalent, htf_live_t_close, IS used for de-dup elsewhere, but the MTF version apparently never got wired into anything), mtf_dir_bull_now/mtf_dir_bear_now (computed, never referenced — the LTF/HTF equivalents are actively used in EMA20 Touch/VWAP Bounce, but MTF's were dead), and a_status_msg_agreement (a plain unused duplicate of a_status_msg, left over from when confirmed vs. agreement message bodies used to differ). Also dropped the unused ID-variable name on 10 plot() calls (plotVWAP, plotEMA200, the 3 touch-dot plots, and 5 ORB session plots) — the plots themselves are fully intact and still draw exactly the same, only the now-pointless "name = " part is gone. Left 3 unused color inputs (col_orb_retest_long/short/fail) untouched — removing an input changes the visible Settings panel and could disrupt anyone's already-saved settings, which is a different kind of risk than internal cleanup and out of scope for a token-budget fix.
// 08/05/2026: v4.03 - CONFIRMED via v4.02 (verified genuinely pasted, changelog present): removing an entire unused request.security() call plus 2 dead booleans plus 10 plot-variable names produced the EXACT SAME 100436 — meaning Pine's compiler was already eliminating that dead code from the counted result before I ever touched it; dead code doesn't cost tokens here any more than comments or tooltips do. Went back to f_market_session_et() with a narrower hypothesis: v4.01's full 4-way hoist (introducing a brand-new mkt_now variable, read from inside f_render_status_table()'s function scope) was the specific problem — reading a top-level variable from inside a function may cost more than calling the function fresh there. This time: NO new variable name, and left the one function-local call (mkt_tbl, inside f_render_status_table()) completely untouched. Instead, reused the existing top-level prox_mkt variable directly for the other 2 top-level sites (orb_r_mkt, a_mkt) — narrower, more conservative test of the same idea, entirely within top-level scope.
// 08/05/2026: v4.04 - v4.03's narrower attempt ALSO made things worse (100436 -> 100445, up by 9) — two independently-designed attempts at consolidating f_market_session_et() calls have now both backfired, by different amounts, even with the second one deliberately avoiding the first one's suspected cause (no new variable, no function-scope crossing). Concluding this specific function is not a valid consolidation target for reasons this session can't diagnose without an actual compiler, and abandoning the idea entirely — reverted orb_r_mkt and a_mkt back to their own independent f_market_session_et() calls, confirmed byte-for-byte identical to the known 100436 baseline (all 4 call sites verified). Not attempting a 3rd variation of this same idea. Next step should be a different category of change entirely, most likely cutting a specific small feature rather than another consolidation attempt, given two-for-two failures on this technique this session.
// 08/12/2026: v4.05 - Token optimization: added a shared f_add_tf_row() helper for the LTF/HTF/MTF row pattern that was hand-written separately 6 times (CBC/EMA cloud/Structure, x2 tables) — each occurrence replaced with one function call. Two adjustments made to the original design: (1) extended the helper with optional tooltip parameters (default "") since the Status table's CBC/Cloud/Structure rows have cell tooltips that the Historical Bar Panel's don't — without this, switching the Status table to the shared helper would have silently deleted those tooltips; Historical's calls simply don't pass them, so its output is unchanged. (2) Changed the helper to accept the LTF/HTF/MTF order array as a parameter instead of computing it internally via f_ltf_htf_mtf_order() — calling that fresh inside the helper would have reintroduced 3x-per-table recomputation, undoing the v3.95 fix that hoisted it to once per table; both tables now pass their already-hoisted tf_order/h_tf_order array in.
// 08/12/2026: v4.06 - Status table label text color (txt_label) changed from a muted slate-gray (#94A3B8) to a light off-white (#F1F5F9) for stronger contrast against the dark navy row background (#1E293B) — per feedback that the row labels (Market, Opening range, EMA cloud, Structure, VWAP, EMA 200) were hard to read. Kept it in the same "slate" color family as the rest of the palette (just much lighter) rather than switching to pure white, for a softer look that's still easy on the eyes. Left the CBC-specific label color (txt_cbc_label, #93C5FD) untouched — it already has strong contrast against its own darker background (#0A1628).
// 08/12/2026: v4.07 - ORB Retest markers split into two distinct colors instead of one shared color for both outcomes: "Success marker color" (green, #00e676 default, same color for both LONG and SHORT) and "Fail marker color" (red, unchanged default). Repurposed two of the three color inputs left unused since v4.02's cleanup pass (col_orb_retest_long/short/fail, originally added but never wired up) — renamed col_orb_retest_long to col_orb_retest_success and dropped col_orb_retest_short entirely, since success no longer needs a direction-specific color under this simpler 2-color scheme. Removed the old single "ORB Retest dot color" input it replaced. Also expanded both marker types' hover tooltips with the full explanation of what each one means (previously just "ORB Retest Success (LONG)" etc., now the complete description of the retest-and-hold vs. failed-round-trip logic). Markers remain hollow squares (bgcolor=color(na)) — only the border color changed.
// 08/13/2026: v4.08 - Confirmed LTF and HTF CBC Flip messages now explicitly state whether the flip happened inside or outside the current session's Opening Range, e.g. "— flip occurred OUTSIDE the Opening Range (above 29875.25)" or "— flip occurred INSIDE the Opening Range (29850.5–29875.25)". Reuses the already-computed OR high/low values (a_or_hi/a_or_lo/a_or_on), precomputed as a plain string (a_or_flip_note) outside f_fire_alerts() per the usual CE10116-avoidance pattern. Applies to both LTF and HTF confirmed flip alerts; left the early/unconfirmed mid-candle variants untouched since the request was specifically about confirmed flips. Empty string (no change to the message) if Opening Range tracking is off for the current session.
// 08/13/2026: v4.09 - Added "Opening Range shading shows" dropdown (Opening Range Sessions group): "After OR closes, until session ends" (the existing default behavior — shading was already persisting the whole session, not disappearing right after the OR closed as an earlier response incorrectly described) or the new "During OR formation only" option, which shows the shade WHILE the OR window itself is still building and removes it the moment that window closes, instead of the reverse. Applies uniformly to all three sessions (NY/London/Asian) via one shared dropdown rather than a separate one per session.
// 08/14/2026: v4.10 - Confirmed HTF CBC Flip messages now distinguish 3 zones relative to the HTF's own EMA9/20, based on the flip's direction: fully agreeing (price already cleared both EMAs the same direction as the flip — silent, no change), partially agreeing (price sitting between the two EMAs — appends "— price inside HTF EMA9/20 zone, partial confirmation"), or fully disagreeing (price hasn't cleared either EMA yet — appends " - ⚠ CAUTIONARY" to the title itself plus a full "⚠ CAUTIONARY SETUP — TREAD LIGHTLY: price still fully above/below HTF EMA9/20" line). Reuses htf_ema9/htf_ema20, already computed elsewhere — no new request.security call. Precomputed as plain strings outside f_fire_alerts(), same CE10116-avoidance pattern as everything else touching that function.
// 08/14/2026: v4.11 - The Zone 2 (partial confirmation) case now also gets a title suffix ("- partial confirmation"), matching how Zone 1 already got "- ⚠ CAUTIONARY" — previously Zone 2 only added the explanation line below the title, leaving the title itself unchanged, which didn't match the requested format.
// 08/14/2026: v4.12 - Zone 1 (cautionary) message now includes a third, explicit directional line: "Caution - Possible long dont short" (SHORT flip case) or "Caution - Possible short dont long" (LONG flip case), added after the existing "⚠ CAUTIONARY SETUP — TREAD LIGHTLY" line. Zone 2 and Zone 3 unchanged.
// 08/14/2026: v4.13 - Added the level's own actual value to every Level Proximity / swing pivot crossing message that was previously distance-only: f_prox_crossing/f_prox_approaching (used for VWAP, Bill Breakers, YDH, YDL, ORB High/Low) and the swing Pivot High/Low Crossing messages now all include ", <Level>: <value>" alongside the existing distance/direction text — e.g. "YDH Approaching: Bearish (0.7 points below, YDH: 4446.5)". Key Level Alert (PP/R1/R2/R3/S1/S2/S3) already included its own value (pi_price) and needed no change — this brings the other alert types up to the same standard for consistency.
// 08/14/2026: v4.14 - Key Level Alert (PP/R1/R2/R3/S1/S2/S3) no longer appends the "Entry Zone / Entry Zone High / Entry Zone Low" (BRSG) lines when it fires on its own. That block was previously attached unconditionally to every confirmed alert routed through the combined bundle, including a pivot crossing with nothing else going on. Added a needs_brsg_line tracker set alongside all 24 OTHER confirmed alert types (A+ Setup, CBC Flip, Agreement, VWAP/EMA200/EMA9-20 Break, Outside HTF ORB, ORB Retest, Price Enters HTF EMA9/20 Zone) but deliberately left unset by the 2 Key Level Alert sites — so if a Key Level Alert happens to bundle with one of those other types on the same bar, the BRSG line still shows (since that other type still wants it), but a standalone Key Level Alert now omits it entirely. The other alert() calls that also use a_brsg_line (Level Proximity, High Confluence Setup, VWAP Bounce, early/unconfirmed bundle) are unrelated to Key Level Alert and were left untouched.
// 08/14/2026: v4.15 - LTF EMA20 Touch - Pullback Entry alert is now capped: only the first 3 touch alerts after each CONFIRMED (bar-close) HTF CBC flip fire — counter resets to 0 on htf_flip_bull/htf_flip_bear (the same confirmed, last-closed-HTF-bar flip already driving the "HTF CBC Flip" alert, htf_cbc_labels_f-based), not the LTF's own flip and not the live/still-forming HTF flip. Once a touch alert fires, the next one is suppressed for the following 3 bars (bar_index-based cooldown), even if it would otherwise still be within the first 3 — so a choppy multi-bar hover on the EMA doesn't spam repeated alerts as if every bar were a fresh "touch". HTF EMA20 Touch alert is untouched — still fires on every qualifying HTF touch with no count/cooldown limit.
// 08/14/2026: v4.16 - "LTF + HTF Agreement - Early (unconfirmed HTF)" now only fires in the LAST 20% of the current, still-forming HTF bar (e.g. minutes 12-15 of a 15m HTF), instead of anytime during that bar's formation. Added htf_live_t_open (this live HTF bar's own open time) by bundling it into the existing htf_live_t_close request.security call (no new call), then gated agree_early_long_ok/agree_early_short_ok on timenow (actual wall-clock time) being past 80% of that bar's open-to-scheduled-close span. "HTF CBC Flip - Early" (the other early/unconfirmed alert type) was deliberately left alone this revision, pending confirmation it should get the same treatment.
// 08/14/2026: v4.17 - Confirmed: "HTF CBC Flip - Early (unconfirmed, still forming)" gets the same last-20%-of-HTF-bar gate added in v4.16, since it's the same category of early/unconfirmed HTF alert. Renamed the v4.16 gate variable from htf_agree_early_time_ok to htf_early_time_ok (no longer Agreement-specific) and added it to flip_early_long_ok/flip_early_short_ok alongside the two agree_early_*_ok booleans. Net effect: neither early/unconfirmed HTF alert type fires until the live HTF bar is in its final 20% — both the standalone Flip-Early bullet and the combined Flip+Agreement bullet now respect the same window.
// 08/14/2026: v4.18 - COMPILE FIX (CE10117: 100344/100256, over by 88) — the LTF EMA20 Touch limiter (v4.15) and the HTF early-alert time gate (v4.16/v4.17) together pushed this over. Two trims, same "fewer declared variables" technique used throughout this file's compile-fix history: (1) inlined ltf_touch_count_ok/ltf_touch_cooldown_ok (each used exactly once, only inside fire_ema20_touch_ltf's own definition) directly into that one boolean instead of as 2 separate named variables. (2) Rewrote htf_early_time_ok from a 2-line float-span-then-division form (with a "span<=0 or ..." guard against dividing by zero) into a single multiplication comparison — (timenow - htf_live_t_open) >= 0.8 * (htf_live_t_close - htf_live_t_open) — which needs no guard at all: a zero/negative span just makes the right side <= 0, and elapsed time is always >= 0, so it's naturally true with no separate branch. Both changes are pure refactors — same inputs, same outputs, verified by tracing both against their pre-v4.18 form line by line.
// 08/14/2026: v4.19 - ATTEMPTED (and reverted): hoisted f_fmt_ny_time(ltf/htf/mtf_last_flip_t) — each called twice (once for its Status table cell, once for the identical alert-email line) — to 3 plain strings computed once. CONFIRMED BACKFIRED: 100311 -> 100355, up by 44, not down. This is the exact same failure pattern as f_market_session_et() (v4.01/v4.03/v4.04) — introducing a brand-new top-level variable that's then read from inside f_render_status_table() costs MORE than the 3 duplicate calls it replaced, for reasons this session still can't fully diagnose without a real compiler. Fully reverted back to 6 direct f_fmt_ny_time() calls (the known-good v4.18 form) before making any further change.
// 08/14/2026: v4.20 - Different approach, avoiding the v4.19 failure pattern: instead of hoisting to a NEW top-level variable (the thing that keeps backfiring), reduced how many times an EXISTING top-level variable (htf_early_time_ok, already added in v4.16/17 and already read from inside f_fire_alerts() before this change) gets read from inside that same function — 4 separate "and htf_early_time_ok" clauses (one per flip_early_long_ok/flip_early_short_ok/agree_early_long_ok/agree_early_short_ok) collapsed into a single wrapping "if htf_early_time_ok" around all 4 declarations plus their bullets. Same cross-scope reference that already existed and already compiled, just 1 occurrence instead of 4 — not a new pattern, so it doesn't carry v4.19's risk. Verified scope-safe: every variable declared inside the new if-block (htf_bull_live_is_revert, flip_early_long_ok, combo_early_long, etc.) is only ever referenced inside that same block; nothing outside it depends on them. Confirmed working: 100311 -> 100300, down by 11.
// 08/14/2026: v4.21 - Still over by 44 (100300/100256). One more small, low-risk trim in the same vein as v4.18's htf_early_time_ok rewrite: ltf_last_touch_alert_bar (v4.15) started at na, requiring a "na(x) or (bar_index - x) > 3" guard so the very first touch alert isn't blocked by a cooldown that's never actually happened yet. Changed the starting value to a sentinel far in the past (-1000000, nowhere near any real bar_index) instead of na, so "bar_index - x > 3" alone is always true before the first real touch — same result, one fewer condition, no na() call. Confirmed working: 100300 -> 100288, down by 12.
// 08/14/2026: v4.22 - Still over by 32 (100288/100256). Applied the same sentinel-instead-of-na trick to 6 PRE-EXISTING de-dup timestamp trackers (not something added this session, but the identical pattern): last_agree_long_htf_close_t, last_agree_short_htf_close_t, last_agree_long_live_htf_t, last_agree_short_live_htf_t, last_htf_flip_live_bull_t, last_htf_flip_live_bear_t. Each was declared "var int ... = na" and checked as "na(x) or (real_timestamp != x)" — since a real HTF close/live-close timestamp is always a huge positive ms-epoch value in this era, it can never equal -1, so switching the starting value (and every place each var gets reset back to "not fired yet") from na to -1 lets the check drop to just "real_timestamp != x", same behavior, no na() call, across all 12 sites (6 vars x reset+compare). Verified each variable is used ONLY inside its own de-dup check/reset — none of them are displayed in a table or read anywhere expecting genuine na semantics — before making this change.
// 08/21/2026: v4.23 - New feature (not a token-trim): added "Shade zone between H/L" fill for the HTF
// Pivot lines, matching the LTF and MTF Pivot rows which already had this. HTF Signals previously only
// drew the resistance/support pivot lines (h_piv_hi_ln/h_piv_lo_ln) with no fill between them. Added
// show_htf_piv_fill + col_htf_piv_fill inputs (same "Shade zone between H/L" label/style as LTF/MTF,
// default off to match MTF), a var linefill h_piv_fill = na, its delete-on-redraw cleanup alongside the
// other h_piv_* line deletes, and the linefill.new(h_piv_hi_ln, h_piv_lo_ln, ...) call right after the
// HTF pivot lines are (re)drawn — identical pattern to the existing l_piv_fill/m_piv_fill blocks. Note:
// MTF already had this fully working (show_mtf_piv_fill, just default-unchecked) — no change needed there.
// This ADDS tokens on top of the still-open CE10117 budget fight (last confirmed count before this: v4.21
// at 100288/100256, v4.22's compile result unconfirmed) — expect the next compile to report a higher
// overage than before; more trimming will likely be needed after this.
// 08/21/2026: v4.24 - Confirmed still over: 100410/100256 (up 154 vs v4.21's last confirmed 100288 —
// the v4.23 HTF-fill feature plus v4.22's unconfirmed change cost more than expected). New technique
// this round, NOT the risky "hoist a brand-new top-level var and read it inside a function for the
// first time" pattern that backfired in v4.19 — instead hoisted byte-for-byte IDENTICAL string
// literals that were being passed to 2-3 separate top-level input()/label.new() calls into a single
// const string, referenced by each call site. This is the exact same shape as the grp_or_sessions /
// grp_pivots / grp_bill_breaker consts already in this file (a top-level literal read by multiple
// top-level input() calls) — same scope in, same scope out, never crossing into a user-defined
// function the way f_fmt_ny_time()/f_market_session_et() did. Six shared consts added: tt_touch_dots
// (EMA20/VWAP/EMA200 touch-dots tooltips, 3x), tt_piv_lines (LTF/MTF/HTF Pivot tooltips, 3x),
// tt_alert_session (Asian/London/NY session-window tooltips, 3x), tt_break_alert (VWAP Break/EMA200
// Break tooltips, 2x), tt_orb_r_success and tt_orb_r_fail (the two long ORB Retest marker label
// tooltips, 2x each). No behavior or displayed text changed — purely deduplicating identical strings.
// 08/21/2026: v4.25 - CONFIRMED via user screenshot: v4.24's string-literal-dedup pass had ZERO
// effect (100410 -> 100410, identical, not even 1 token saved). Important finding: Pine's CE10117
// token count is almost certainly counting lexical/syntax tokens (identifiers, operators, keywords,
// each string literal as ONE token regardless of its character length) — NOT raw character count.
// That means a duplicated "tooltip=\"...\"" (2 tokens: the named-arg identifier + one string-literal
// token) replaced by "tooltip=shared_const" (2 tokens: named-arg identifier + one identifier-reference
// token) plus one new top-level "const string shared_const = \"...\"" declaration is a wash — you save
// nothing, you just move the same token count around. Retracting that as a technique; no more tooltip/
// string hoisting after this. New, different, higher-confidence technique instead: removed redundant
// "if not na(x)" guards immediately wrapping a *.delete(x) call, across ~32 sites (the barstate.islast
// pivot/flip-line cleanup block, the brsg else-branch cleanup, and all six ORB session H/L label
// cleanup blocks for NY/London/Asian). This is safe because Pine's *.delete() functions (line/label/
// box/linefill) already treat a na id as a no-op — proven by this file's OWN pre-existing code: the
// "if new_day" reset block (~line 970) already calls line.delete()/label.delete() on ydh_line, ydh_lbl,
// etc. completely unguarded, no na-check, and that block already compiles today. So "if not na(x) /
// x.delete(x) / x := na" was always redundant vs. just "x.delete(x) / x := na" — removing the guard
// actually deletes real tokens (the if/not/na/parens), unlike the string-hoist which just relocated
// them. No behavior change: same lines get deleted at the same times either way.
// 08/21/2026: v4.26 - New feature (adds tokens, not a trim): "Max Loss Per Trade" — a checkbox +
// points input in Stop Management. When enabled, if the LTF Trailing Stop's distance from the
// current close exceeds the configured points, the LTF+HTF Agreement entry alert (both the
// confirmed and early/live variants) is skipped for that signal — i.e. the trade is treated as
// too risky to take. Scoped ONLY to LTF+HTF Agreement (agreement_enter_long/short and their _live
// counterparts) — deliberately NOT applied to LTF/HTF CBC Flip or A+ Setup alerts, both because
// Agreement is this file's clearest "trade entry" signal tied to the same LTF-based stop the
// Stop Management section already manages, and because gating those other alert types would mean
// reading this new state from inside f_fire_alerts() where A+ Setup's booleans are defined — see
// below for why that's avoided. Implementation deliberately keeps the whole check at top-level
// scope: a new "max_loss_ok" bool (computed from close and the already-top-level htf_trail_stop)
// is folded directly into agreement_enter_long/short/_live's own formula, so f_fire_alerts() keeps
// reading the exact same identifiers it always did — no new distinct external reference added to
// that function. This matters because this file's own v3.87 changelog found that a function's
// compiled cost scales with the SET of distinct external names it references, not how many times
// each is read (that's what forced moving the entire a_status_msg-building block out of
// f_fire_alerts() back then) — and this session independently rediscovered the same effect twice
// (f_fmt_ny_time in v4.19, f_market_session_et historically) when hoisting a brand-new top-level
// var into a first-time read inside an existing function. Net effect on the CE10117 count is
// unknown until compiled — flagging plainly that this is a real feature addition on top of the
// still-open token deficit, not another trim.
// 08/23/2026: v4.27 - New feature (adds tokens, not a trim): "HTF EMA 200 Break" — a higher-
// timeframe version of the existing LTF "EMA 200 Break" alert, plus wired into the two other
// places the user asked for. htf_ema200 added as a 3rd return value on the EXISTING HTF EMA9/20
// request.security() tuple (no new security() call, which is the genuinely expensive thing to
// duplicate). (1) Alerts: new alert_on_htf_ema200_break input, fires "HTF EMA 200 Break: Bullish/
// Bearish" the same way the LTF version does — mirrors a_e200_break_bull_now/bear_now/enter_bull/
// enter_bear exactly, just against htf_ema200 instead of ema200; only one new external reference
// added to f_fire_alerts() (htf_ema200 itself). (2) A+ Setup - Major Pivot Proximity: new
// aplus_check_htf_ema200 checkbox, wired into the top-level aplus_pivot_note block (outside
// f_fire_alerts() entirely — zero function-scope risk). (3) Level Proximity Alerts: new
// prox_check_htf_ema200 checkbox, added as a 10th slot to the existing lp_vals/lp_names/lp_enabled
// array-loop (was 9) rather than a new sequential if-block — that loop already exists specifically
// to avoid duplicating this exact pattern for token budget reasons. (4) Chart: new "HTF EMA 200"
// toggle + color under HTF Signals, plotted as a plain line (separate from the existing HTF EMA
// cloud fill, which stays 9/20 only).
// 08/23/2026: v4.28 - Confirmed via screenshot: v4.27 compiles at 100514/100256, over by 258 (v4.26's
// Max Loss feature + v4.27's HTF EMA200 feature cost more than v4.25's na-guard cleanup saved). Found
// the single biggest remaining win in the file: "and alert_session_ok" was appended to EVERY individual
// alert-firing condition inside f_fire_alerts() — 44 occurrences total. Collapsed both blocks it
// appeared in down to one wrapping "if alert_session_ok" each (same proven pattern as htf_early_time_ok
// in v4.20, just at much larger scale): the big "confirmed_titles" block (LTF/HTF Flip through HTF
// Pullback Entry, 34 occurrences removed) and the "early_titles" block (LTF Flip Early + the existing
// htf_early_time_ok sub-block, 6 occurrences removed) — 40 removed total, 2 kept (one wrapping if per
// block). Verified safe: "if any_confirmed"/"if any_early" below each block only ever go true from
// conditions that already required alert_session_ok, so wrapping them too is behaviorally identical,
// not a new restriction — confirmed zero remaining functional alert_session_ok reads outside those 2
// wrapping ifs and its own top-level definition. Untouched: alert_on_startup (already correctly NOT
// gated by alert_session_ok, per its own comment) and the EMA20 Touch alerts (also intentionally
// ungated, per their existing comment) — neither had "and alert_session_ok" to begin with.
// 08/23/2026: v4.29 - CONFIRMED via screenshot: v4.28's alert_session_ok collapse actually worked —
// 100514 -> 100338, saved 176. Still over by 82. This round's fix is smaller: found 4 more of the
// exact same redundant "if not na(x) / x.delete(x) / x := na" pattern already fixed for line/label/

// 08/25/2026: v4.30 - ORB Retest & Retest redesign (feature/rename, not a token-trim): the two-outcome
// scheme (Retest success / Retest Fail) is now THREE outcomes with a consistent +/- convention (+ =
// breakout attempt originated ABOVE the ORB, - = originated BELOW, held across all three families):
// "ORB Pass +/-" (renamed from "ORB Retest / Break & Base" — unchanged detection, orb_r_enter_long/
// short), "ORB Flip +/-" (renamed from "ORB Retest Fail" — unchanged detection, orb_r_fail_up/dn, the
// full round-trip-through-the-opposite-side case), and NEW "ORB Fail from +/-" (orb_r_timeout_up/dn) —
// price broke out but never confirmed OR flipped before the "Max bars to wait for retest" window
// expired, ending up stuck back inside the range. Timeout is checked after the Flip check and only
// depends on orb_r_broke_up/dn still being true, so a same-bar Flip naturally takes priority (Flip
// already resets orb_r_broke_up/dn to false first) and Timeout is single-fire the same way Flip and
// Pass already are. Added col_orb_retest_stall (new marker color input, amber) and tt_orb_r_stall (new
// tooltip) alongside the existing success/fail ones (kept the old variable names tt_orb_r_success/
// col_orb_retest_success for "Pass" and tt_orb_r_fail/col_orb_retest_fail for "Flip" rather than
// renaming them, since v4.24 already confirmed renaming/hoisting strings doesn't move the token count
// either way — only the string CONTENTS changed). New timeout markers plot at the same up/dn offset as
// the Pass marker on their own side (Fail-from+ at mk_up_y like Pass+; Fail-from- at mk_dn_y like
// Pass-), distinguishing them from Flip's opposite-side placement (which marks where the reversal
// happened, not where the attempt started). Dashboard (index_with_FAQ_*.html) updated in lockstep for
// the new/renamed alert title strings — see that file's own changelog.
// 08/25/2026: v4.31 - Fix: Level Proximity alerts (VWAP/EMA20/EMA200/HTF EMA200/Bill Breakers/
// YDH-YDL/ORB High-Low/Swing Pivots) could fire twice within a couple minutes of each other for the
// same level — the existing visit-latch (prox_latched_name) only suppresses repeats while price
// stays continuously in the same Crossing/Approaching band; if price wobbles right across the
// Approaching-distance boundary (default 3.0pts) the bullet briefly goes empty, which resets the
// latch, so the very next bar back inside the band reads as a fresh "new visit" and refires — even
// though it's really the same visit. Added a hard floor on top of the latch: new input
// prox_cooldown_bars ("Min bars between repeat alerts (same level)", default 5) plus a var
// prox_last_alert_bar tracker and prox_cooldown_ok boolean, both computed at TOP LEVEL and folded
// directly into level_alert_new_visit's own definition — f_fire_alerts() itself is untouched, still
// reading the same level_alert_new_visit it always did, so this adds zero new external references
// inside that function (the pattern that's repeatedly proven costly for the CE10117 token budget).
// 08/25/2026: v4.32 - Dropped the "Bullish"/"Bearish" word from every Level Proximity alert message
// (VWAP/EMA20/EMA200/HTF EMA200/Bill Breakers/YDH-YDL/ORB High-Low/Swing Pivot Crossing) — it read as
// a directional trade call, but it was always just "is price currently above (Bullish) or below
// (Bearish) this level right now", not an actual signal. Confusing in practice (e.g. "ORB Low
// Approaching: Bearish" reads like a short setup, when it's really just noting price is below ORB
// Low while approaching it). The distance + "points above/below" wording already says the same thing
// unambiguously, so the word is simply gone now: e.g. "ORB Low Approaching: Bearish (3 points below,
// ORB Low: 4732.6)" -> "ORB Low Approaching (3 points below, ORB Low: 4732.6)". Also dropped the now-
// unused bull_bear locals in f_prox_crossing/f_prox_approaching and the bb_ph/bb_pl locals in the two
// pivot-crossing blocks (pure token-count bonus, not the point of this change). IMPORTANT: this also

// isolate the level name for the visit-latch. Dashboard (index_with_FAQ_*.html) updated in lockstep —
// see that file's own changelog.

// 08/29/2026: v4.41 - Reworked the "In FVG of HTF Flip Candle" alert's firing behavior per follow-up
// request: (1) now fires intrabar — switched alert.freq_once_per_bar_close to alert.freq_once_per_bar
// so it no longer waits for the LTF bar to close before alerting; (2) throttled with a new input

// 08/29/2026: v4.42 - Extended the Confluence Score from 5 factors to 6, per a follow-up request for
// additional low-token confluences. (1) The old "BRSG" factor is now "Zone" and counts EITHER a BRSG

// past the last confirmed LTF swing low/high (l_last_lo/l_last_hi, already computed for the Pivot
// table rows) but closes back on the flip's side of it, agreeing with the current direction — a
// classic liquidity-sweep/stop-hunt-then-reversal read. (3) Both the Zone and Sweep factors are only
// counted within a new adjustable window ("Zone/Sweep Factors: Valid Within N LTF Bars of HTF Flip",

// 08/29/2026: v4.43 - Added a 7th Confluence Score factor: HTF Structure Break (BOS/CHoCH). Sits on
// top of the existing htf_hi_struct/htf_lo_struct HH/LH/HL/LL classifier with a new persistent trend
// state (htf_struct_trend) — a fresh Higher High while the trend is already Bullish (or a fresh Lower
// Low while already Bearish) is a BOS (continuation); the same pivot while the trend was the OPPOSITE
// direction is a CHoCH (reversal), which is what actually distinguishes this from the pre-existing
// "HTF Pullback Entry - Structure Confirmed" alert's HH+HL/LH+LL check (that one only ever reads the
// current static pivot labels, it never tracked whether this pivot broke or continued the prior
// trend). The factor counts a recent break in the CURRENT direction (cbc) within
// confluence_recency_ltf_bars LTF bars (reused from v4.42, not a new input) via two "bars since last
// break" counters. Per explicit request, this is the ONLY one of the 7 factors with its own
// include/exclude toggle ("Include HTF Structure Break (BOS/CHoCH) in score") — the other 6 stay
// always-on for now; whether to retrofit them the same way is still an open decision, deferred until
// this addition's real-world token cost is known. When toggled off: excluded from the score sum, its
// ✓/✗ line disappears from the alert breakdown, and the alert message's denominator drops from /7
// back to /6 (confluence_max_score, computed dynamically off the same toggle) — so the message never
// misleadingly claims "X/7" when only 6 factors are actually in play. Alert threshold input's maxval
// raised 6->7. No new request.security call, no new loop — the trend state and break counters are
// plain variables layered onto pivot detection that was already running every bar.

// 08/29/2026: v4.50 - Three changes per follow-up. (1) Confluence Score factors Flip(LTF) (cf_f1_flip)
// and HTF Flip Recency (cf_f9_htf_flip) switched from a fixed bar-count recency cap (<=5 LTF bars /
// <=htf_flip_recency_bars HTF bars) to "valid for the whole post-flip life, until the next reverse
// flip" — a new persistent latch per side (ltf_has_flipped, htf_has_flipped) is set true on the flip
// bar and stays true through every subsequent bar in that direction; it only gets re-armed (still
// true, for the new direction) when the opposite flip occurs, so there's no more N-bar expiry window
// during which a still-valid, unreversed flip stopped counting. The now-unused htf_flip_recency_bars
// input was removed (its tooltip on confluence_use_htf_flip updated to describe the new behavior)
// rather than left dangling in the Settings panel. (2) HTF Pullback Entry's recent flip window
// (htf_pullback_recent_bars) was already defaulting to 4, matching the requested value — confirmed,
// no change needed. (3) "Include LTF Trailing Stop in emails" (alert_show_stop) default flipped from
// true to false per follow-up screenshot showing it unchecked.

// 08/29/2026: v4.55 - Per follow-up ("Info seems redundant... distinguish the counted ones by
// underlining them"): Pine table cells don't support underline or any per-substring/rich text styling
// at all — text_color and bgcolor apply to a WHOLE cell, never to individual characters or words
// inside one string, so literal underlining of specific factors within a packed-string row isn't
// something the table API exposes. Implemented the closest real equivalent instead — chosen after
// presenting 3 options (per-factor cells needing a table restructure, an Info row that only shows
// mismatches, or dropping Info entirely): a new dedicated small table (f_render_confluence_cells(),
// same separate-table pattern this file already uses for Pivot Points), one cell per confluence
// factor, each with its own color: green (st_bull) = counted toward the score right now; amber
// (new st_amber color, #B45309) = the factor's raw touch condition IS true but it's excluded — a
// recency window or its own include/exclude toggle is currently zeroing it out; gray (st_neutral) =
// not true at all. This subsumes the old Info row's whole job (surfacing "true but not counted") via
// color instead of a duplicate row, so the Info row is gone. The Status table's Confluence Score
// entry collapsed from 3 rows down to 1 simple "S:n/max" row (status_row_count contribution back to
// 1); the old packed-string breakdown variables (cf_core_reasons_str_tbl, cf_extended_reasons_str_tbl,
// cf_f7/8/9/10_reasons_part_tbl, cf_info_reasons_str) were removed as dead code. New inputs
// (Confluence Score group): confluence_use_cells_table (show/hide toggle, default true) and
// confluence_cells_position (own position dropdown, default "Bottom right" so it doesn't default-
// overlap the main Status table). Unaffected by any of this: confluence_reasons_str/a_status_msg
// (still full factor names, used by every alert) and the separate always-visible Fib Zone row added
// in v4.54 (still shows the actual 50%/61.8% price levels) — the new Fib cell here is an additional,
// different display (counted/excluded/false), not a replacement for either.
// 08/29/2026: v4.56 - Compile fix: v4.55's f_render_confluence_cells() referenced tbl_txt_sz, but
// that variable is local to f_render_status_table()'s own scope — Pine user-defined functions don't
// share local variables with each other, so it showed as CE10272 "Undeclared identifier" at compile
// time (caught by the user, not by this environment, which cannot compile-test Pine). Fixed by
// computing a local conf_txt_sz inside f_render_confluence_cells() via the existing top-level
// f_table_text_size() helper (reusing status_table_text_size, same input the main table already
// uses) — identical to how the Pivot Points table already computes its own local piv_txt_sz the same
// way. Purely a scope fix; no behavior or display change beyond making it compile.
// 08/29/2026: v4.57 - Compile fix (2nd, same root cause): the v4.56 fix used a `replace_all` text
// substitution to swap `text_size=tbl_txt_sz, text_halign=text.align_center` -> `..conf_txt_sz..`
// across the whole file, intending to only touch the 10 new confluence-factor cells — but the Status
// table's own HEADER/title cell ("MapleStax CBC ...") ALSO happens to use
// text_halign=text.align_center, and got caught by the same blanket substitution, breaking a

// declared) into a reference to conf_txt_sz (which doesn't exist there) — a second CE10272 undeclared
// identifier, caught by the user again. Reverted that one line back to tbl_txt_sz. Confirmed via grep
// this was the only other match: exactly 10 "text_size=conf_txt_sz" remain, all inside
// f_render_confluence_cells(), and exactly 1 "text_size=tbl_txt_sz, text_halign=text.align_center"
// remains, which is the (correct) header cell. No other display or behavior change.
// 08/29/2026: v4.58 - Confluence Factors table row count made configurable per follow-up (was
// hardcoded to 2 rows of 6/4). New input confluence_cells_rows (Confluence Score group, default 1,
// range 1-10) sets how many rows the 10 factor cells spread across; columns are derived automatically
// as ceil(10/rows) so the grid always fits exactly. Rewrote the function's cell-placement from 10
// hardcoded table.cell() calls into 3 parallel arrays (text/color/tooltip, fixed factor order:
// Flip/HTF/Cloud/Level/Zone/Sweep/Structure/Volume/HTFFlip/Fib) plus a for-loop that fills left-to-
// right then wraps (row = i / conf_cols, col = i % conf_cols) — relies on Pine's documented int/int
// division and modulo both being integer (truncating) when both operands are int, which this
// environment cannot compile-test directly but is standard, documented Pine type-coercion behavior.
// Also added proper re-creation handling (delete + rebuild) when either confluence_cells_position or
// the resolved row count changes — a table's column/row count is fixed at table.new() time and won't
// auto-resize, same reason f_render_status_table() already re-creates itself on its own settings

// largest single addition this session — a brand-new per-bar detection+array-tracking subsystem, on

// this could push the file back over budget; genuinely can't know without a real compile.

// since. htf_has_flipped itself was removed as dead code (its only reader was factor #9). No direction
// check was added (matches the old behavior — any unfilled post-flip gap counts, bull or bear).

// (HTF/Cloud/Level/Zone) cells in the Confluence Factors table. Pine's table API has no per-cell
// border — table.new()'s border_width/border_color draw uniform gridlines between EVERY cell, and
// frame_width/frame_color only wrap the table's own outer edge; neither can ring an arbitrary subset
// of cells. Flagged to the user in-chat with the real options (e.g. a second table just for these 4,

// confluence_alert_threshold and the same green/gray coloring rule already used by the Status table's
// own Confluence Score row — per follow-up ("where is the confluence score at the beginning"). The
// table is now 11 cells total (was 10); confluence_cells_rows' maxval bumped 10->11 and its tooltip
// updated to match. (2) The HTF/Cloud/Level/Zone group (the "four" from two follow-ups ago) is now
// bracketed with "[" prepended to the HTF cell's text and "]" appended to the Zone cell's text — a
// text-based visual approximation of a border, since (as disclosed in the v4.68 entry above and again
// in-chat) Pine's table API genuinely cannot draw a border, outline, or distinct color around an
// arbitrary subset of cells: border_width/border_color are uniform across every internal gridline of
// the whole table, and frame_width/frame_color only wrap the table's own outer edge. A real border
// would need a second, separate table (whose own frame IS a real border), but Pine's 9 fixed table
// anchor positions can't place a second table pixel-adjacent to this one — at the same corner they'd
// overlap instead of sitting side by side. Went with the bracket approximation rather than asking a
// third time, since the request had already been made twice; open to switching to the two-table
// approach (accepting the positioning risk) or a different visual (e.g. a shared background tint)
// if the brackets don't read well once compiled. Only cosmetic/display changes — no signal logic
// touched, confluence_score's own value and cf_f1.._f10 identifiers unchanged.
// 08/30/2026: v4.70 - Follow-up ("change xHF to xHFV"): the Confluence Factors cells table's factor
// #9 abbreviation changed from "HF" to "HFV". Purely a text-literal change in the conf_texts array.from(...)
// call in f_render_confluence_cells() — no logic, tooltip, or scoring change.
// 08/31/2026: v4.71 - Follow-up: Flip (LTF), confluence factor #1, is now its own include/exclude
// toggle (confluence_use_flip), OFF BY DEFAULT per the request ("can we have an input so it can be
// included or excluded from the confluence score - default is off"). This is a real behavior change:
// out of the box the Confluence Score no longer counts Flip(LTF) at all, and confluence_max_score
// defaults to 9 (was 10) until the toggle is switched on. Implementation mirrors the existing
// Structure/Volume/HTFFlip/Fib pattern exactly: cf_f1_flip_touch is the raw signal (renamed from the
// old always-on cf_f1_flip), cf_f1_flip = confluence_use_flip and cf_f1_flip_touch is what actually
// counts. confluence_max_score's base dropped from 6 to 5 (the now-permanently-always-on factors are
// just HTF/Cloud/Level/Zone/Sweep) with a new "+ (confluence_use_flip ? 1 : 0)" term added alongside
// the other 4 toggle terms. The alert-text breakdown (cf_core_reasons_str) now splices in a new
// cf_f1_reasons_part string that goes empty when the toggle is off — same "conditionally-empty
// appended piece" pattern as cf_f7/8/9/10_reasons_part — so a disabled Flip cleanly disappears from
// the alert text instead of showing "✗Flip(3)" forever. The Confluence Factors cells table's Flip
// cell now uses the same 3-way green/amber/gray rule as every other toggleable factor (amber = the
// LTF did flip, but the toggle is currently off) instead of the old always green-or-gray. The app's
// own confluence grid (buildConfluenceFactorsHtml in the dashboard) needed NO change — it parses
// whatever ✓/✗ tokens are actually present in the alert text, so it already picks up a missing Flip
// segment and the new denominator automatically.
// 08/31/2026: v4.72 - Two follow-ups. (1) "same for key level confluence" — Level (factor #4) gets
// the exact same include/exclude treatment as Flip (LTF) just got in v4.71: a new confluence_use_level
// toggle, OFF BY DEFAULT. cf_f4_level_touch is the raw signal (renamed from the old always-on
// cf_f4_level), cf_f4_level = confluence_use_level and cf_f4_level_touch is what counts.
// confluence_max_score's base drops from 5 to 4 (the now-permanently-always-on factors are just HTF/
// Cloud/Zone/Sweep) with a new "+ (confluence_use_level ? 1 : 0)" term. The alert-text breakdown gets
// a new cf_f4_reasons_part (same conditionally-empty pattern as cf_f1_reasons_part) so an excluded
// Level cleanly disappears from the text instead of always showing "✗Level". The Confluence Factors
// cells table's Level cell now uses the same green/amber/gray rule as every other toggleable factor.
// Out of the box, BOTH Flip and Level are now excluded — confluence_max_score defaults to 8/8 (was
// 10/10 before v4.71). (2) Alert threshold (confluence_alert_threshold) default changed from 4 to 3,
// per follow-up — existing charts that already have this input saved keep their own saved value;
// only fresh applications of the indicator (or an explicit "reset settings") pick up the new 3.
// 08/31/2026: v4.73 - Follow-up with a screenshot of the compiled v4.72 settings panel ("set default
// as in image for what to include in confluence score") — changed the DEFAULT (not the current value
// of any input already on a chart) of 3 more toggleable Confluence Score factors to match exactly
// what the screenshot showed checked/unchecked: confluence_use_structure_break true->false,
// confluence_use_volume true->false, confluence_use_fib true->false. confluence_use_htf_flip stays
// true (already checked in the screenshot) and confluence_use_flip/confluence_use_level stay false
// (already unchecked, set in v4.71/v4.72). Net effect: out of the box, only HTF Flip Recency is now
// an "on" toggleable factor — combined with the 4 always-on core factors (HTF/Cloud/Zone/Sweep), a
// fresh application of this indicator now defaults to a 5-factor Confluence Score
// (confluence_max_score = 5 out of the box) against the alert threshold of 3 set in v4.72. Every
// factor can still be switched back on individually — this only changes what a brand-new chart
// starts with, exactly as confirmed by the screenshot; it does not touch any input already saved on
// an existing chart (same caveat as the v4.72 threshold-default change).
// 08/31/2026: v4.74 - Two follow-ups. (1) confluence_use_htf_flip default changed true->false, per
// follow-up ("by default off HTF Flip Recency") — this was the last toggleable factor still on by
// default, so out of the box ALL 6 toggleable factors (Flip, Level, Structure, Volume, HTFFlip, Fib)
// are now off, and confluence_max_score defaults to 4 (just the always-on core: HTF/Cloud/Zone/
// Sweep) against the alert threshold of 3 set in v4.72 — meaning a fresh chart's alert now fires on
// 3 of 4 core factors agreeing, before any optional factor is switched back on. (2) Full reorder of
// the Confluence Score display per an explicit new sequence: core 4 (HTF/Cloud/Zone/Sweep) stays as
// the lead, followed by the toggleable tail in this exact order: Volume, Fib, Structure, Level,
// Flip(LTF), HTFFlip — notably moving Level OUT of its old spot between Cloud and Zone (where it had
// stayed since v4.42 even after becoming toggleable in v4.72) and Flip(LTF) OUT of its slot before
// Sweep, down into the toggleable tail alongside the other 4 optional factors, since all 6 are now
// equally off-by-default toggles. Applied consistently to both display surfaces: the alert-text
// breakdown (cf_core_reasons_str trimmed to just the 4 core factors; cf_f1_reasons_part and
// cf_f4_reasons_part switched to the same "leading  , no trailing space" format as
// Structure/Volume/HTFFlip/Fib so all 6 toggleable segments can be freely reordered in
// cf_extended_reasons_str) and the Confluence Factors cells table (conf_texts/conf_colors/conf_tips
// reordered to match). The "[" "]" bracket markers moved WITH the core group — they now wrap HTF
// through Sweep (cells 0-3) instead of the old HTF/Cloud/Level/Zone grouping, since Level is no
// longer adjacent to the other 3. Flagged to the user in-chat in case that's not the bracket
// placement wanted now that Level has moved. confluence_score's own sum and confluence_max_score's
// formula are both order-independent and unaffected — this is a pure display-order + defaults change.
// ====================================================================================

enum RefLineStyle
    solid = "Solid"
    dashed = "Dashed"
    dotted = "Dotted"

f_ref_line_style(RefLineStyle s) =>
    switch s
        RefLineStyle.dashed => line.style_dashed
        RefLineStyle.dotted => line.style_dotted
        => line.style_solid

// ═══════════════════════════════════════
//  PIVOT POINTS (Daily, Standard/Floor formula)
//  PP = (H+L+C)/3, R1 = 2*PP-L, S1 = 2*PP-H, R2 = PP+(H-L), S2 = PP-(H-L),
//  R3 = H+2*(PP-L), S3 = L-2*(H-PP) — computed from the PRIOR completed daily bar.
//  Distinct from the existing "Pivot (LTF/HTF/MTF)" rows, which are swing high/low fractals
//  (ta.pivothigh/pivotlow), not this classic floor-trader formula.
// ═══════════════════════════════════════

show_pivot_r1 = input.bool(true, "R1", inline="pivshow1", group="Pivot Points")

// ORB Retest / "Break & Base" — the classic ORB playbook: (1) an initial breakout beyond the OR high/low
// that you should NOT chase, (2) a pullback that RETESTS the broken level from the other side (old
// resistance becomes support, or vice versa — the "base"), (3) a bar closes back in the breakout
// direction, confirming the base. Tracks whichever session's ORB (Asian/London/NY) is currently active.
show_orb_retest_markers = input.bool(true, "Show markers on chart", group=grp_orb_retest, tooltip="Plots a small triangle at the bar where a")
orb_retest_tolerance = input.float(0.15, "Retest zone (× OR range)", minval=0.01, maxval=1.0, step=0.01, group=grp_orb_retest, tooltip="How close price must come back to the ORB")
orb_retest_min_break = input.float(0.10, "Min breakout distance (× OR range)", minval=0.0, maxval=2.0, step=0.01, group=grp_orb_retest, tooltip="Minimum clear-past-level distance")
orb_retest_max_bars = input.int(60, "Max bars to wait for retest", minval=1, group=grp_orb_retest, tooltip="How many bars after the initial breakout")
col_orb_retest_success = input.color(#00e676, "Pass marker color", inline="orbr_col", group=grp_orb_retest, tooltip="Color for the 'ORB Pass' marker (+ or - — same color either way).")
col_orb_retest_fail = input.color(color.red, "Flip marker color", inline="orbr_col", group=grp_orb_retest, tooltip="Color for the 'ORB Flip' marker — full round-trip through the opposite side before confirming.")
col_orb_retest_stall = input.color(#ff9800, "Fail from marker color", inline="orbr_col", group=grp_orb_retest, tooltip="Color for the 'ORB Fail from' marker — stalled/timed-out back inside the range, never confirmed or flipped.")
// (No "hole color" input needed — the inner circle uses color.new(_, 100), full transparency,
// so it shows through to whatever's actually behind it regardless of chart theme/background.)
show_any_or = or_ny or or_london or or_asian

// Returns [0,1,2] (index 0=LTF, 1=HTF, 2=MTF) reordered so the SMALLEST actual timeframe renders
// first, regardless of which conceptual tier (LTF/HTF/MTF) it belongs to — e.g. if HTF is ever
// configured smaller than the chart's own LTF, HTF still renders first. Used identically by the
// CBC, Structure, and EMA cloud row groups (all three share the same three timeframe assignments,
// so this only needs computing once, not separately per group), in both the Status table and the
// Historical Bar Panel.
f_ltf_htf_mtf_order() =>
    array<int> minutes = array.from(int(timeframe.in_seconds() / 60), int(timeframe.in_seconds(htf_timeframe) / 60), int(timeframe.in_seconds(mtf_timeframe) / 60))
    array.sort_indices(minutes, order.ascending)

// Shared LTF/HTF/MTF table row helper — replaces the near-identical tier-loop block that used to be
// hand-written separately for CBC/EMA cloud/Structure, in BOTH the Status table and the Historical
// Bar Panel (6 copies total). Tooltip params default to "" (Pine's table.cell default too) so the
// Historical panel's calls — which never had cell tooltips — behave identically without passing them;
// the Status table's calls pass its existing tooltip text so nothing is lost by switching to this.
f_add_tf_row(table tbl, int start_row, string label_base,
             string ltf_msg, color ltf_bg,
             string htf_msg, color htf_bg,
             string mtf_msg, color mtf_bg,
             bool show_ltf, bool show_htf, bool show_mtf, bool show_mtf_field,
             string chart_tf, string htf_tf, string mtf_tf,
             color label_bg, color label_txt, color on_txt, string txt_sz,
             array<int> order,
             string ltf_tt = "", string htf_tt = "", string mtf_tt = "") =>
    int r = start_row
    for i = 0 to 2
        int tier = array.get(order, i)
        if tier == 0 and show_ltf
            table.cell(tbl, 0, r, label_base + " (" + chart_tf + ")", bgcolor=label_bg, text_color=label_txt, text_size=txt_sz, text_halign=text.align_left, tooltip=ltf_tt)
            table.cell(tbl, 1, r, ltf_msg, bgcolor=ltf_bg, text_color=on_txt, text_size=txt_sz, text_halign=text.align_right)
            r += 1
        else if tier == 1 and show_htf
            table.cell(tbl, 0, r, label_base + " (" + htf_tf + ")", bgcolor=label_bg, text_color=label_txt, text_size=txt_sz, text_halign=text.align_left, tooltip=htf_tt)
            table.cell(tbl, 1, r, htf_msg, bgcolor=htf_bg, text_color=on_txt, text_size=txt_sz, text_halign=text.align_right)
            r += 1
        else if tier == 2 and show_mtf and show_mtf_field
            table.cell(tbl, 0, r, label_base + " (" + mtf_tf + ")", bgcolor=label_bg, text_color=label_txt, text_size=txt_sz, text_halign=text.align_left, tooltip=mtf_tt)
            table.cell(tbl, 1, r, mtf_msg, bgcolor=mtf_bg, text_color=on_txt, text_size=txt_sz, text_halign=text.align_right)
            r += 1
    r

// Builds the sorted field-render order once. Returns an array of field-id strings (e.g. "market",
// "cbc", ...) in the order they should actually be drawn, per the Order inputs above. Same order
// used by both the Status table and Historical Bar Panel.
f_field_render_order() =>
    array<string> ids = array.from("market", "price", "cbc", "or", "cloud", "vwap", "ema200", "structure", "lastflip", "stop", "entryzone", "chart", "pivltf", "pivhtf", "pivmtf", "fliplevel", "confluence", "fibzone")
    array<int> orders = array.from(order_market, order_price, order_cbc, order_or, order_cloud, order_vwap, order_ema200, order_structure, order_lastflip, order_stop, order_entryzone, order_chart, order_pivltf, order_pivhtf, order_pivmtf, order_fliplevel, order_confluence, order_fibzone)
    array<int> sorted_idx = array.sort_indices(orders, order.ascending)
    array<string> sorted_ids = array.new<string>()
    for i = 0 to array.size(sorted_idx) - 1
        array.push(sorted_ids, array.get(ids, array.get(sorted_idx, i)))
    sorted_ids
